# Whole-repository read, the cockpit pass (uncapped) — 2026-10-02

**What this is.** The third part of the read the Owner asked for: `apps/desktop` — the Rust crates, the frontend and the cockpit's own documents — re-read with no cap on findings. It follows [`WHOLE_REPO_READ_2026-10-01.md`](WHOLE_REPO_READ_2026-10-01.md) (capped, 932 findings) and [`WHOLE_REPO_READ_2026-10-02.md`](WHOLE_REPO_READ_2026-10-02.md) (the engine, tools and documents, uncapped). It is a list of findings, not of fixes: **every item below is OPEN.**

> **Found while closing these, 2026-10-08 — not one of the 1370.** `Memory.writeRecord.test.tsx` failed
> on CI run 37821123738 and passed here. Three of its four panel lookups were a bare
> `document.querySelector` straight after `select`, racing the row's own record-state read; the fourth
> had been fixed for exactly this after run 35468281628. Reproduced on demand by delaying that read
> 400 ms: the old file fails those three, the new one passes all ten. All four go through one waiting
> helper now.

## How it was run

- **35 slices** of at most 300 KB cut from the 527 text files under `apps/desktop` (9.1 MB; images, fonts, lockfiles and generated schemas excluded), one read-only reader per slice, plus **four sweeps**: duplicated Rust, documents against code, dead code and placeholders, tests that do not test.
- Every finding went to a second agent, 25 at a time, told to refute it by opening the cited line: **1780 confirmed, 82 refuted, none left unverified**. Of the confirmed, 410 were already in one of the two earlier records and are left out. **1370 remain** and are listed below.
- **The readers did not finish every file.** They named 168 files they opened and did not read end to end — most of the large Rust sources among them. The list is §4. A finding count from a file on that list is a floor, not a total, and "nothing was reported" about such a file means nothing.
- No reader ran `cargo`, `npm` or a CI kit. "Confirmed" means a second agent agreed after opening the line: ◑, not ✅. **The Builder reproduced none of these.**
- The working tree moved under the readers: five pull requests merged while they ran (`#325`–`#329`). None changed a source file under `apps/desktop`; one changed comments in `provision/src/audit_signer.rs`.
- The full text of each finding — evidence, the verifier's note, the other location of a duplicate — is in [`archive/whole_repo_read_3_2026-10-02.json`](archive/whole_repo_read_3_2026-10-02.json), keyed by the `R3-` id used here.

## The numbers

- By severity: 2 high · 80 medium · 1288 low.
- By kind: 324 stale-claim · 236 bug · 218 duplicate · 211 dead-code · 177 test-defect · 154 design-contradiction · 42 security · 8 other.

## 1. High

### R3-0001 · `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/governed_turn_ipc.rs:229` — A committed reply longer than 8192 bytes cannot be delivered: the reply frame uses the request cap

- Evidence: CommittedMessage.body is 'the exact ... accepted-output bytes' (output cap MAX_OUTPUT_BYTES = 8_388_608), but broker/src/main.rs:708-710 does `encode_frame(&body)` which refuses > MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD_BYTES = 8192, and src/governed_turn.rs:24 caps the read at 8192+64. The turn commits, then the connection fails.
- Second agent: Read end to end: ladder_executor.rs:403 pulls output up to MAX_OUTPUT_BYTES (8_388_608), verify_and_accept commits it, then broker/src/main.rs:708-710 serializes the whole result and calls encode_frame, which refuses > MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD_BYTES=8192 (ipc_framing.rs:58); the client also caps at 8192+64 (src/governed_turn.rs:24). No body bound exists in broker_orchestrator/governed_verification, and the default max_output_tokens is 4096, so a committed reply over ~8 KB fails the connection after co…
- Suggested fix: Give the broker->renderer reply its own bound sized for a full committed body (or chunk it), on both the broker write and the app read, and add a test with a body over 8 KiB.

### R3-0002 · `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/repo.rs:2725` — Scheduler panics on a trigger ending in a multibyte character and poisons the DB mutex (found checking the TS 'port')

- Evidence: `rest.split_at(rest.len() - 1)` is a BYTE index: `every: 5м` gives a 3-byte `5м`, index 2 is not a char boundary, so it panics inside run_due while lib.rs:460 holds `state.db.lock()`. commands.rs:27 `locked` refuses a poisoned mutex; create validates length only. Not run, read only.
- Second agent: repo.rs:2725 is `rest.split_at(rest.len() - 1)` on a byte length; run_due_halves (:2782) calls it for every ENABLED automation while lib.rs:460 holds `state.db.lock()`, create_automation checks length only, and Cargo.toml sets no panic=abort. A scratch rustc copy of the function (not the app, no cargo) panicked on `every: 5м` with 'end byte index 2 is not a char boundary' and left a held std Mutex poisoned; commands.rs `locked` refuses a poisoned mutex. Precondition: the automation must be arme…
- Suggested fix: Split on the last char (`rest.chars().last()` / `char_indices`) and add a non-ASCII trigger case to the core tests.

- **R3-0066** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/tcb_integrity.rs:114` — The TCB floor's docs say writability is decided by a POSIX ACL scan; the only production probe reads no ACLs *Evidence:* tcb_integrity.rs:113-114 'In production these come from an fstat ... plus an ACL/group scan'; :19 'by mode bits, POSIX ACL, or group'. The only FsProbe, broker/src/tcb_probe.rs:196-200: 'POSIX ACLs beyond the mode bits are not read here; that is a known narrowing'. *Fix:* State in the module header and on FileFacts that ACLs are not read by the shipped probe, or read them in LinuxFsProbe.
- **R3-0067** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json:40` — Bundle longDescription tells the user ordinary chat runs through the Claude CLI; the default build refuses *Evidence:* longDescription: 'ordinary chat runs through the Claude CLI in a private sandbox — contained, but NOT governed'. resolve_provider (ai.rs:443-500) returns Err with no allow flag; apps/desktop/README.md:17 says 'the default build refuses to generate'. *Fix:* Change the shipped description to say AI is refused by default and the CLI is a development opt-in. **CLOSED 2026-10-08 (bundle description)** — it says this build refuses to generate until a provider is configured and that the Claude CLI is a development opt-in; `resolve_provider` read to confirm (`None` with no allow flag is `Err`). NOT changed: `docs/brand/readme/gate-light.svg` / `gate-dark.svg` and the README alt text still say `contained — REACHED · Ordinary chat runs through the Claude CLI provider` without the opt-in. That drawing is the Owner's, and its lines are laid out by hand.
- **R3-0068** `apps/desktop/src/features/Decisions.strings.ts:147` — The sealed/unreachable body says the engine evidence chain 'is not exposed to the desktop yet' *Evidence:* sealedBody en: 'The engine evidence chain is read-only and is not exposed to the desktop yet.' The page itself calls desktop.readEvidenceChain → `read_evidence_chain` (src-tauri/src/governance.rs:709); a `blocked` reply means the engine was reached and refused. chainSealedAnnounce (line 36) repeats… *Fix:* State what happened (the engine refused, or was not reached) instead of claiming the read path does not exist. **CLOSED 2026-10-08, with the finding's own evidence corrected** — the copy now says the read could not be made (refused, or engine not reached) instead of saying the path does not exist. NOT taken from this finding: that `blocked` means the engine was reached. `tools/check_capabilities.py` records, from reading tauri 2.11.5, that `read_evidence_chain` is registered outside the ACL manifest and the window is refused it (that is R3-0054); neither has been settled by running the app, so the copy asserts neither.
- **R3-0069** `apps/desktop/src/features/Integrations.strings.ts:300` — Copy says the desktop 'can declare and enable connectors' and that Enable 'records your intent in the local registry'; the T-052 gate refuses every status write *Evidence:* capBody: "So it can declare and enable connectors but cannot test one." enableActionNote (l.162): "Records your intent in the local registry." Backend: set_status returns Invalid{approval: required} unless a natively confirmed grant exists, and nothing mints one. *Fix:* Reword the three strings to say enabling needs an approved grant, or wire the approval request so the sentence becomes true. **CLOSED 2026-10-08 (copy), the gap itself OPEN** — the three strings no longer say the desktop can enable a connector, and a refused status change is announced with its reason. Confirmed in the backend: `integrations::set_status` spends a grant in BOTH directions and the only non-test caller of `approvals::create` raises a run-step request, so a connector can be neither enabled nor DISABLED in the shipped app. `automations::set_enabled` gates arming only and says why a stop button must never be gated; the connector gate does not follow that. Changing the gate or adding a request command is T-052 code and waits for an audited change.
- **R3-0070** `apps/desktop/src/features/Notifications.strings.ts:43` — 'Ranked intake · sorted by priority' is false: the feed is rendered in store order, newest first *Evidence:* inboxNote en: 'sorted by priority · pick a band to filter'; inboxHeading 'Ranked intake'. Backend: core/src/repo.rs:1104 `SELECT * FROM notifications ORDER BY created_at DESC`. *Fix:* Either sort `visible` by unread then SEVERITY_ORDER (the comparator `lead` already uses) or reword the heading and note to 'newest first'. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — the heading is `Intake` and the note says `newest first`, in three languages; tests hold all three to making no priority claim.
- **R3-0071** `apps/desktop/src/features/Onboarding.strings.ts:27` — First-run copy says local actions 'run directly and reversibly'; marking a notification read has no inverse and delete_automation is a hard DELETE *Evidence:* howBody: 'Local actions (notes, tasks, notifications, automations) run directly and reversibly.' build.rs:43-45 lists only list_notifications / mark_notification_read (no un-read); core/src/repo.rs:2858 `DELETE FROM automations WHERE id = ?1` behind a native confirm (commands.rs:2569). *Fix:* Drop 'and reversibly' (in en/hy/ru) or name which local actions actually have an undo. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — the copy says local actions run directly on this machine and that some cannot be undone, in three languages; reproduced first.

### bug (27)

- **R3-0003** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/audit-signer/src/register.rs:359` — apply() ends with `sc.exe start` against a binary that has no service dispatcher, so registration can never return Ok *Evidence:* register.rs:359 `run_sc(&["start"...])?` accepts only exit 0/1073/1056. bin/service.rs main() is `std::process::exit(real_main())` with no StartServiceCtrlDispatcher (grep: zero hits in the crate). provision/tests/audit_signer.rs:1381 says so: "`sc.exe start` cannot succeed against it". *Fix:* Implement the SCM entry point (StartServiceCtrlDispatcherW + status reporting) in service.rs, or stop apply() and the module docs from claiming a startable service.
- **R3-0004** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/broker/src/ladder_executor.rs:199` — The ladder's content source blocks every conversation that already contains a reply: it refuses the role the desktop stores *Evidence:* read_window: `if !HISTORY_ROLES.contains(&role.as_str()) { return Err(UpstreamBlocked) }`; HISTORY_ROLES = ["user","assistant","system"] (governed_prepare.rs:117). The messages table only admits MESSAGE_ROLES = ["user","agent","system"] (domain.rs:15); replies are written role "agent" (commands.rs:… *Fix:* Map the stored `agent` role to `assistant` in read_window the way commands.rs:1571 does, and test it against rows written through repo::chat::post_message.
- **R3-0005** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/launcher/src/main.rs:653` — Journal gate entries VerifyEntry/VerifyUnprivileged are recorded by bare statements, not by the checks they name *Evidence:* `journal.record(Step::VerifyEntry);` (653) and `journal.record(Step::VerifyUnprivileged);` (672) are standalone. Deleting `verify_launcher_fd_set(..)?` (623) or `verify_final_state(..)?` (671) leaves the journal identical, yet privilege_drop.rs:245-249 says listing the gates makes that deletion vis… *Fix:* Make the verifiers return a token that is the only way to record the gate step (or record inside a wrapper that calls the verifier), and add a test that fails when the call is removed.
- **R3-0006** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/audit_signer.rs:556` — The ledger DACL plan is never applied, so verify_installed step 4 could not pass on a real install *Evidence:* register.rs:311 'let _ledger_plan = spec::ledger_dacl_plan(app_sid, &signer_sid)?;' (discarded). Only non-test apply_dacl callers: register.rs:342 (signer_dir) and anchor.rs:1137. verify_ledger_custody (L808) refuses a ledger dir that is not SE_DACL_PROTECTED. *Fix:* Apply ledger_dacl_plan to the ledger directory in a non-test path (or drop the read-back step), and test that verify_installed's step 4 is reachable.
- **R3-0007** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/audit_signer.rs:1291` — install_steps ends with `sc.exe start` of a binary that is not a Windows service *Evidence:* L1291 'sc.exe start {SIGNER_SERVICE_NAME} # the service MINTS ITS OWN KEY on first start'. ci.yml:690: '`brops-audit-signer.exe` is a plain console binary with no StartServiceCtrlDispatcher, so `sc.exe start` cannot succeed against it'. grep finds no StartServiceCtrlDispatcher in audit-signer/. *Fix:* Implement the SCM service protocol in the signer binary, or state in the module header and install_steps that the plan cannot be executed today.
- **R3-0008** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/audit_signer.rs:1503` — One signed-but-not-installed anchor makes check_monotonic refuse that ledger for ever *Evidence:* L1503 'if fields.previous_anchor_sha256.as_deref() != Some(prev.anchor_sha256.as_str()) { return Err(AnchorChainBroken'. audit-signer/src/lib.rs:208 saves state before replying; bro_audit_log.py:612-615 signs then installs. A reply lost between the two leaves no branch that accepts the older on-dis… *Fix:* Keep the previous emitted digest as well and accept a request naming it at the same or higher count, or document the administrator reset as the only recovery.
- **R3-0009** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:2514` — The 9 MiB stdout cap truncates silently and turns a long agent turn into a timeout *Evidence:* `let capped = tokio::io::AsyncReadExt::take(stdout, MAX_STDOUT_BYTES);` — past the cap next_line yields None, the loop breaks, and `timeout_at(deadline, child.wait())` waits on a child blocked writing a full pipe until the 900 s deadline, then returns "claude CLI timed out". Same shape at 2801. *Fix:* Detect that the cap was reached and return a size-limit error (or keep draining and discarding) instead of treating it as EOF.
- **R3-0010** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:2553` — On timeout or CLI failure the trust-surface revert is reported only to stderr *Evidence:* `return Err("claude CLI timed out")` (2553, 2612), size limit (2578) and non-zero exit (2621) leave the guard to Drop, which does `let _ = self.settle();` — notice goes to eprintln only. Line 2535 itself says "a revert the owner cannot see is one they cannot judge". *Fix:* Settle explicitly on every error return and append the notice to the Err string (and to on_delta).
- **R3-0011** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2092` — stream_run_step claims the step and consumes the approval grant before it resolves the provider *Evidence:* claim_step_for_execution runs at :2092; crate::ai::provider_is_governed() is first asked at :2141 and `Err(e) => fail_attempt!(e)`. A deterministic config error (no allow flag set) therefore spends the native-confirmed grant and terminally fails the step ('no retry of a failed step exists'). *Fix:* Resolve the provider (and the unconfigured pre-flight) before claim_step_for_execution so a configuration refusal costs nothing.
- **R3-0012** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs:2125` — A failed run-step attempt fails the step but not the run, so the next call executes the following step *Evidence:* fail_attempt! calls only repo::runs::fail_step_execution (step -> failed) and returns; no advance, no fail_step_and_run. next_runnable_step (repo.rs:2096) picks the next 'pending' step, so a later stream_run_step runs past a failed step. The Rejected arm (:2017) fails step AND run. *Fix:* Make fail_attempt! fail the run atomically with the step (fail_step_and_run semantics) or call advance so the run terminates.
- **R3-0013** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/governed_selftest.rs:183` — The trust self-test can never show PASSED: the UI gates on production_verified, which is always false in-process *Evidence:* governed_selftest.rs:183 `production_verified: outcome.production_verified`; proof.rs:86 'always `false` here', asserted at :524. TrustSelftest.tsx:42 `passed = available && bound && production_verified`; there is no branch for done && available && !passed, so a good Windows run renders nothing. *Fix:* Expose `chain_bound` (or `may_post_as_demonstration_verified()`) from the command and have the panel gate on bound && chain_bound, with a visible failed branch. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — the command returns `chain_bound` and the panel gates on `bound && chain_bound`, with a visible did-not-verify branch; reproduced first, two mutants killed. The Windows arm of the Rust change is not compiled on any runner.
- **R3-0014** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/governed_turn.rs:24` — A committed governed reply larger than ~8 KB cannot be returned: 8192-byte reply frame vs 8 MiB output *Evidence:* MAX_REPLY_BYTES = MAX_FRAME_PAYLOAD_BYTES(8192)+64. The reply carries `message.body` (governed_turn_ipc.rs:235); output may be 8_388_608 bytes (governed_output_pull.rs:149). broker/src/main.rs:709 `encode_frame(&body)` fails AFTER run_governed_turn committed, so the renderer gets broker_transport_f… *Fix:* Size the renderer reply frame for the maximum body (or chunk it) and make an over-size reply a blocked verdict decided before commit.
- **R3-0015** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json:29` — `targets: all` ships rpm/AppImage/dmg bundles that have no anchor installer, so their first launch always refuses *Evidence:* :29 `"targets": "all"`; only `bundle.linux.deb` (:48-53) carries `postInstallScript` and `/usr/lib/brops/brops_install_anchor`. anchor.rs:275-297 refuses to create an anchor on any non-Windows platform, lib.rs:423 `provision_local_trust(&dir)?` aborts startup; release.yml:81 builds macos-latest. *Fix:* Restrict Linux targets to deb (and drop macOS) until those formats carry the root installer.
- **R3-0016** `apps/desktop/src/components/aios-shell.css:108` — `var(--slow) var(--spring)` expands to two easing functions, making five transition/animation declarations invalid *Evidence:* aios.css:54-55 `--slow:220ms cubic-bezier(.2,.6,.2,1)`, `--spring:cubic-bezier(.16,1,.3,1)`. aios-shell.css:108,141,187 `transform var(--slow) var(--spring)`; 167 `animation: navPop var(--slow) var(--spring)`; 206 `animation: stageEnter var(--slow) var(--spring) both`. One easing per item is the gr… *Fix:* Write an explicit duration with `var(--spring)` (e.g. `220ms var(--spring)`) in all five places. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — all five read `220ms var(--spring)`. Measured in real Chromium on this machine, which now has the browser: with the old declarations `.stage-enter` computes `animation-name: none`, with the fix `stageEnter` at 0.22s, `navPop` on the active icon, and a 0.22s transform transition on the mark. A jsdom test expands the tokens and refuses two easings in one item across every stylesheet under `src/` (it found exactly these five), and `stage-enter` is in `MOTION_CLASSES`. The earlier note here gave the missing browser as the reason not to fix; the six Chromium specs pass, 434 tests. What nobody has done is LOOK at the page-enter animation: it has never been on a screen.

- **R3-0021** `apps/desktop/src/features/Conversations.tsx:469` — Unmounting a group thread cancels the consensus deck's in-flight ask on the same room *Evidence:* cleanup: `void desktop.cancelReply(conversation.id)` on every unmount. ai.rs:334 request_cancel: "Request cancellation of EVERY in-flight turn armed under `key`" (key = conversation id). L('blockedBody')}` = "A governed turn for this agent was halted by the wall. Its result is withheld until a verified receipt is produced." The only writer of agents.status is repo::seed: `("shield", "blocked")`, run at startup (lib.rs:426). *Fix:* Show the raw status and say the desktop does not know why, or drop the panel until a real governed turn can set this status. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — same change as R3-0047; the panel stays and no longer narrates a halted turn or a withheld result.

L834 and L844 call renderBlocked(reason, fix) with no title, so the default L('blockedByWall') = 'Blocked by the wall' heads a refusal where nothing was called. *Fix:* Pass L('refusalTitle') as the title for the contract-refusal and standing-seal panels instead of prefixing it into the reason under the wall title. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — the page's own contract refusal and its standing seal are headed `Refused by the run contract`, not `Blocked by the wall`; reproduced first.

L1025 says of the same store: 'a refused read — not the wall, and not something a mode, scope or approv… *Fix:* Title store denials as a store refusal (as was done for list_automations) and drop the approval advice. **PARTLY REFUTED 2026-10-08, not changed** — `automations::set_enabled` DOES demand an approval grant (`core/src/repo.rs`, `require_and_consume` with `AUTOMATION_ENABLED_ACTION_TYPE`), so `request approval` is not false advice for enabling. What is still unanswered is which command would create that grant, and whether `run` and `delete` denials deserve the same title. **UPDATE 2026-10-08, later the same day** — the open question is answered: NO command creates that grant (the only non-test `approvals::create` is for a run step), so `request approval` named a step that cannot be taken. Files.tsx:147 repeats "the engine scope guard refused… *Fix:* Reword the sealed state to name the desktop files-root confinement (or remove it) and stop attributing the refusal to the engine. **CLOSED 2026-10-08** — the sealed hint says the desktop refused the open and that the engine is not consulted on this route; three languages, test-held. `read_file` re-read: `confine` + `read_text`.

The comparison is on the RAW ratio now', citing 4.4996 and 4.4995. *Fix:* Compare `u.ratio < u.need` on the raw ratio in all three places, as line 311 of the same file already does. **CLOSED 2026-10-08 in the code, the Chromium run is CI's** — all three filters and the planted-defect check go through `belowNeed`, which is `!(ratio >= need)`: the raw ratio, and a ratio that is not a number fails instead of passing (it compared false both ways). ### security (9)

- **R3-0056** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/broker/src/main.rs:342` — Shipped broker builds the §2.5 floor's principal set with the three shapes the proof driver's copy names as the defect; ALREADY drifted *Evidence:* main.rs:345 '.map(|m| m.values().filter_map(|v| v.as_u64().map(|u| u as u32)).collect()).unwrap_or_default()'. tcb_verify.rs:73-78: filter_map, unwrap_or_default and 'as u32' each 'REMOVES a principal from the set ... a floor asked about nobody passes'. *Fix:* Move the strict principals() parser into brops_broker and have main.rs refuse a uids block that is not stated whole.
- **R3-0057** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/agent_bundle.rs:250` — Capability enforcement is self-declared: covers() checks what a step says it requires, and the runner writes without checking any capability *Evidence:* L124-126 '`capabilities` ... enforced: a step may not require a capability the grant withholds'. covers() L251-256 only compares step.requires to the grant. repo.rs:3201-3228 Store arm calls knowledge::create with no WRITE_LOCAL check; grep WRITE_LOCAL in repo.rs hits only test fixtures. *Fix:* Derive the required capability from the step kind (Store => WRITE_LOCAL) at run time and refuse when the grant lacks it, instead of trusting step.requires.
- **R3-0058** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/core/src/key_manifest.rs:414` — check_and_persist compares against the caller's in-memory floor and rewrites the file every call, so a stale process lowers a floor another process raised *Evidence:* L419-420: `check_and_advance(floor, manifest)` then unconditional `write_floor_atomically(path, ...)`. broker/src/manifest_resolver.rs:197,384 holds the floor in a Mutex loaded once. Same epoch+hash returns Ok, so an old process rewrites {5,h} over an on-disk 7. *Fix:* Re-read the on-disk floor under an exclusive lock inside check_and_persist and refuse/skip the write when disk is already at or above the value being written.
- **R3-0059** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/provision/src/anchor.rs:581` — The custody probes use fixed file names and read a planted unwritable file as 'directory not writable' *Evidence:* L581 'let probe = dir.join(PROBE_NAME); match std::fs::File::create(&probe)' and L870 '.brops-custody-probe-parent'; PermissionDenied => refusal recorded. Reproduced: a mode-000 file of that name in a directory I can write makes O_CREAT|O_TRUNC|O_WRONLY return EACCES. *Fix:* Probe with create_new on an unpredictable name (as posix_euid already does) and treat AlreadyExists as unmeasurable.
- **R3-0060** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:1257` — BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS omits engine/config, where the trusted-key registry lives *Evidence:* The list has engine/runtime, engine/schemas, engine/contracts, engine/laws only. engine/config/trusted-keys.json and protected-control-plane.json exist and are unprotected; this file's own test (line 4827) names `engine/config/trusted-keys.json` as the registry that would answer. *Fix:* Add engine/config to BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS and to the required list in the_trust_surface_covers_the_things_that_decide_what_verified_means.
- **R3-0061** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:1257` — BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS omits engine/tools, which holds the receipt signer and supervisor *Evidence:* engine/tools/ has brops_receipt_signer.py ("the ONLY component that holds the receipt-signing private key"), brops_signer_service.py, brops_supervisor_service.py, brops_supervisor_attest.py, broctl.py. Only the repo-root `tools` entry is listed; engine/tools is writable by a model turn and never sn… *Fix:* Add engine/tools to BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS.
- **R3-0062** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:1257` — The guard's own source file is not on the protected list *Evidence:* BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS lists src/engine_trust.rs, governance.rs, governed_turn.rs, governed_selftest.rs but not apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs, which defines the list, TrustSurfaceGuard and tool_args. A turn can empty the list in source; it takes effect on the next build. *Fix:* Add apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs (and lib.rs, which sets BROPS_PROJECT_DIR) to BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS.
- **R3-0063** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:1257` — The wall's hooks and the gates' configuration are outside the protected surface *Evidence:* Doc (1251) protects "what PROVES the other three: the CI gates in `tools/`, the workflows in `.github/`". Not listed: .claude/hooks/canonical_law_gate.py, coordination_stop_guard.py, .claude/settings.json, and config/ (required-checks.json, canon-budget.json, spec-conformance.json) that the gates r… *Fix:* Add .claude/hooks, .claude/settings.json and config to BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS.
- **R3-0064** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ai.rs:3925` — A test pins commands.rs as writable although it holds the first production-gate refusal *Evidence:* `for open in ["apps/desktop/src", "apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands.rs"] { assert!(!BRO_PROTECTED_PATHS.contains(&open) ...` — commands.rs:1408 defines governed_verification_unconfigured() and GOVERNED_TRUSTED_MANIFEST_PROVISIONED, refusal 1 in CLAUDE.md §6. *Fix:* Move the provisioning probe into a protected module (or protect commands.rs) and drop the assertion that it must stay writable.
